# 35 Apple II and Apple IIgs game projects: reviewed source discoveries

Review 35 Apple II/IIgs game projects and preserve documented holds

Date: 2026-10-07 · Review: ready

Snapshot check: all pinned proofs match the supplied expected commits.

### Identity (reviewed)

Jordan Mechner explicitly identifies this as his 1985–89 Apple II Prince of Persia code. MASTER and CTRL implement game startup, save/load, character controls and environmental interactions; original levels and image tables are also in the complete tree.
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/README.md
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/01%20POP%20Source/Source/MASTER.S
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/01%20POP%20Source/Source/CTRL.S

### Classification (reviewed)

An original-author source archive, not a disassembly or inferred port. It preserves game implementation and historical support files, rather than merely launching a disk through an emulator.
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/README.md
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/01%20POP%20Source/Source/CTRL.S

### Source CPU (reviewed)

The author explicitly states 6502 assembly. The reviewed boot and control files use 6502 A/X/Y registers, zero-page pointers and Apple II soft switches; no 65816-native implementation is claimed.
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/README.md
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/01%20POP%20Source/Source/BOOT.S
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/01%20POP%20Source/Source/CTRL.S

### Target CPU (reviewed)

The preserved assembly emits the original 6502 Apple II program. IIgs model detection in the boot code concerns compatible execution of this 8-bit program, not a native IIgs rebuild.
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/01%20POP%20Source/Source/BOOT.S
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/01%20POP%20Source/Source/EQ.S

### Build (reviewed)

The archive includes assembly modules, include directives, fixed origins, level data, graphics and disk routines. BOOT.S ends with a historical SAV directive; no turnkey modern build script exists in the inspected complete tree. Toolchain and disk-layout reconstruction would still need to be established. Limitations: Static discovery only. No source was built, executed, played, hardware-tested or byte-compared; independent verification flags remain null.
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/01%20POP%20Source/Source/BOOT.S
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/01%20POP%20Source/Source/MASTER.S
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/01%20POP%20Source/Source/EQ.S

### Runtime profiles (reviewed)

BOOT.S calls check128k, checks the IIe/IIc/GS identity and auxiliary-memory behavior, and displays a 128 KB IIc/IIe requirement on failure. This directly supports the original boot-path RAM requirement; broader compatibility remains untested.
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/01%20POP%20Source/Source/BOOT.S
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/01%20POP%20Source/Source/EQ.S

### AI (no-evidence-found)

No development-AI attribution was found in the inspected README, license, source headers or observed head commit. This limited negative finding does not establish non-use.
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/README.md
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/LICENSE
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/01%20POP%20Source/Source/CTRL.S

### Relationships (reviewed)

No original Apple II archive lineage appears in the prior-root/current-source union. The catalogue’s SDLPoP entry is a DOS-derived C implementation of the same title, and Macintosh Prince of Persia 2 is a separate sequel/implementation. LICENSE refers to the README: Mechner allows study and experimentation but expressly does not grant Prince of Persia franchise rights; this is not recorded as a blanket open-source license.
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/README.md
- https://github.com/jmechner/prince-of-persia-apple-ii/blob/25243550b6e1d035d7b51b5d7e5114a83ebcb5c0/LICENSE

### Identity (reviewed)

Bill Budge’s repository names the Apple II Pinball Construction Set. RUN2 identifies PCS pinball control and implements player/ball handling; RUN implements flippers, bumpers, ball motion and scoring. This is game-level evidence despite the empty README.
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/source_disc2/RUN.S
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/source_disc1/RUN2.S
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/LICENSE
- https://github.com/billbudge/PCS_AppleII

### Classification (reviewed)

Original pinball game and construction-editor source. Editor functions alone are not the basis for inclusion: the inspected runtime directly simulates balls, collisions, flippers, targets, score and multi-player state.
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/source_disc2/RUN.S
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/source_disc1/RUN2.S
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/source_disc1/EDIT.S

### Source CPU (reviewed)

RUN and RUN2 are 6502 assembly with A/X/Y registers, carry arithmetic and indexed/indirect addressing. The Apple II boot and I/O conventions distinguish this archive from Budge’s other platform versions.
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/source_disc2/RUN.S
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/source_disc1/RUN2.S
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/source_disc2/BOOT2.S

### Target CPU (reviewed)

The preserved fixed-address assembly targets the Apple II’s 6502 execution environment. No 65C02-only, native 65816 or modern host output was evidenced.
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/source_disc2/BOOT2.S
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/source_disc2/RUN.S

### Build (reviewed)

Nine assembly files are divided into source_disc1 and source_disc2. ORG/OBJ directives, boot-sector reads, editor/runtime code and embedded bitmaps are preserved. README is empty and no build script, assembler-version instruction or disk image appears in the complete tree, so a reproducible build procedure is unresolved. Limitations: Static discovery only. No source was built, executed, played, hardware-tested or byte-compared; independent verification flags remain null.
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/source_disc2/BOOT2.S
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/source_disc2/RUN.S
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/source_disc1/EDIT.S
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/README.md

### Runtime profiles (no-evidence-found)

The source establishes Apple II graphics, input and disk boot behavior, but the inspected material gives no dependable model, RAM or DOS minimum. No hardware requirement is inferred just from the game’s age.
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/source_disc2/BOOT2.S
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/source_disc2/RUN.S
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/source_disc1/RUN2.S

### AI (no-evidence-found)

No AI-development disclosure was found in the empty README, MIT license, inspected source headers or observed original-source commit. AI usage remains unknown.
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/README.md
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/LICENSE
- https://github.com/billbudge/pcs_appleii/blob/615edfe43b7747ae9d7a390066b4ec0738950b53/source_disc1/RUN2.S

The original Apple II repository is new to the normalized-root and title/lineage screen. This is distinct from the indexed Bill Budge 3-D graphics module. The repository carries an MIT permission grant with Bill Budge’s 1982 copyright.

The repository identifies two original Bilestoad source/data disks and attributes the game to Datamost. BACK contains gameplay routines for movement, player swapping, weapons, wounds, death and scoring, not just extracted graphics.

Historical source preservation from original disks, accompanied by extracted listings. It is not represented as a new reverse-engineered recreation, and the disk images are kept as the archive’s preferred unconverted material.

### Source CPU (reviewed)

BACK and TWO use 6502 accumulator/index operations, zero-page and indexed indirect addressing, decimal-mode score arithmetic and Apple II graphics/speaker addresses including $C030 and $C054–$C057.

The retained original Apple II assembly is a 6502 target. A modern host or IIgs-native 65816 version is not evidenced.

### Build (reviewed)

Original DSK images are preserved alongside LISA assembler-era ICL chains, origins and extracted source/data files. The archived Apple II game source emits 6502 machine code. Included Atari-named media share a broad CPU family but do not change this selected source profile or justify an additional tested target.

The Modem Text Version directory has chained PHOBOS source modules, tables and system messages plus a preserved DSK image. Actual code reads modem status at $C0A6; a plain local-console build is not assumed. This pass did not reconstruct the assembler/disk workflow or inspect the entire alternative Phobio implementation. ### Identity (reviewed)

Jennifer L. Schmidt’s AppleIIGames repository collects games she wrote as a teenager in the 1970s. Three complete individual listings were implementation-reviewed: Basketball implements paddle/hoop collisions, timed matches, team scores and replay; Country Drive implements obstacles, paddle steering/speed, lap scores, collision penalties and a three-crash ending; Tag implements keyboard-controlled players, obstacles, role reversal, pickups, six tagging rounds and a winner.
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/README.md
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/LICENSE
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/lores/BASKETBALL.int
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/lores/COUNTRY%20DRIVE.int
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/lores/TAG.bas

### Classification (reviewed)

One original-author source collection, with three named games supported by individually inspected listings. The shared repository is the project unit; the listings do not establish separately maintained projects. Binary-only hi-res disks and unreviewed anthology entries are not promoted by association.
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/README.md
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/LICENSE
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/lores/BASKETBALL.int
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/lores/COUNTRY%20DRIVE.int
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/lores/TAG.bas

### Source CPU (reviewed)

The reviewed BASIC sources target the original Apple II and include direct I/O accesses and POKE-installed 6502 speaker instructions, including LDA $C030. This establishes a 6502 implementation from embedded code and the explicit machine target, rather than from BASIC syntax alone.
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/README.md
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/LICENSE
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/lores/BASKETBALL.int
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/lores/COUNTRY%20DRIVE.int
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/lores/TAG.bas

### Target CPU (reviewed)

These preserved listings execute through the appropriate Apple II BASIC interpreter plus embedded 6502 instructions. No port to a new ISA or native 65816 build is established for this reviewed collection.
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/README.md
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/LICENSE
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/lores/BASKETBALL.int
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/lores/COUNTRY%20DRIVE.int
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/lores/TAG.bas

### Build (reviewed)

The author provides the complete plain-text Basketball, Country Drive and Tag listings and a lo-res disk image. Basketball and Country Drive use Integer BASIC; Tag uses Applesoft BASIC. No compiler is inherently required, but no listing was run or compared with disk contents. Basketball line 740 branches to nonexistent line 2550, while its listing ends with 2250; this potential exit-path defect is retained without repair or a claim of tested failure. Limitations: Static discovery only. No source was built, executed, played, hardware-tested or byte-compared; independent verification flags remain null.
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/README.md
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/LICENSE

- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/lores/COUNTRY%20DRIVE.int
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/lores/TAG.bas

### Runtime profiles (reviewed)

Basketball requires Integer BASIC and two paddle inputs PDL(0)/PDL(1). Country Drive requires Integer BASIC, paddle 0 and a paddle button, with a keyboard quick-stop option. Tag requires Applesoft BASIC and two keyboard movement-key sets. Low-resolution graphics and speaker access are explicit in all three. Exact RAM and ROM-version minima remain unspecified.
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/README.md
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/LICENSE

- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/lores/COUNTRY%20DRIVE.int
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/lores/TAG.bas

### AI (no-evidence-found)

The inspected author README, ISC license and all three complete game listings contain no AI-development disclosure. AI usage remains unknown; the historical dates do not by themselves establish non-use in preservation work.
- https://github.com/bikibird/appleiigames/blob/5ab6216020d011bbf26419af0f920a66458db4cb/README.md

### Relationships (reviewed)

The three named BASIC games are retained as subjects of one author repository-root collection, consistent with the catalogue’s author-archive convention. The indexed Atari 2600 Basketball is unrelated to Schmidt’s Integer BASIC game. No same-code lineage or prior author-root review was found. The repository has an ISC license; no rights to unrelated same-title games are implied.

### Identity (reviewed)

Stefan Wessels rewrote Manic Miner for Apple II in 2020. The native source includes player movement, enemies, level progression, collision, title/UI and twenty levels; a 2026 single-screen variant sits beside the scrolling game.
- https://github.com/stewbc/mminer-apple2/blob/eabbc96695afda8ce8806e7da64a0c6624f50252/README.md
- https://github.com/stewbc/mminer-apple2/blob/eabbc96695afda8ce8806e7da64a0c6624f50252/src/apple2/mminer.asm
- https://github.com/stewbc/mminer-apple2/blob/eabbc96695afda8ce8806e7da64a0c6624f50252/src/apple2/game.inc
- https://github.com/stewbc/mminer-apple2/blob/eabbc96695afda8ce8806e7da64a0c6624f50252/src/mmm/src/mmm.c

### Classification (reviewed)

Native assembly remake, explicitly described as a rewrite from the ground up. The separate SDL2 Manic Miner Machine executes an embedded game through a 6502 CPU core and Apple II memory/IO callbacks; that wrapper is audit-only and is not a second native port.
- https://github.com/stewbc/mminer-apple2/blob/eabbc96695afda8ce8806e7da64a0c6624f50252/README.md
- https://github.com/stewbc/mminer-apple2/blob/eabbc96695afda8ce8806e7da64a0c6624f50252/src/apple2/mminer.asm
- https://github.com/stewbc/mminer-apple2/blob/eabbc96695afda8ce8806e7da64a0c6624f50252/src/apple2/game.inc
- https://github.com/stewbc/mminer-apple2/blob/eabbc96695afda8ce8806e7da64a0c6624f50252/src/mmm/src/mmm.c

### Source CPU (no-evidence-found)

The README explicitly identifies the ZX Spectrum Manic Miner as the game being remade. No Spectrum binary or Z80 source was inspected here, so source_cpu and source_language remain empty; the native 6502 rewrite belongs to the target side.
- https://github.com/stewbc/mminer-apple2/blob/eabbc96695afda8ce8806e7da64a0c6624f50252/README.md

### Target CPU (reviewed)

README explicitly identifies 6502 assembly and ca65. The native mminer.asm and game.inc contain the Apple II program entry and direct 6502 game routines; this is not inferred from the Apple II name.
- https://github.com/stewbc/mminer-apple2/blob/eabbc96695afda8ce8806e7da64a0c6624f50252/src/apple2/mminer.asm
- https://github.com/stewbc/mminer-apple2/blob/eabbc96695afda8ce8806e7da64a0c6624f50252/src/apple2/game.inc
- https://github.com/stewbc/mminer-apple2/blob/eabbc96695afda8ce8806e7da64a0c6624f50252/src/mmm/src/mmm.c
- https://github.com/stewbc/mminer-apple2/blob/eabbc96695afda8ce8806e7da64a0c6624f50252/Makefile
- https://github.com/stewbc/mminer-apple2/blob/eabbc96695afda8ce8806e7da64a0c6624f50252/Makefile-noscroll.mk

### Build (reviewed)

Makefiles cover ca65/cl65 game and ProDOS loader builds, disk packaging with AppleCommander/Java, and a separate non-scrolling build. The author calls the game complete, but neither variant was rebuilt. The SDL2 wrapper is outside the native build judgment. Limitations: No compilation, launch, gameplay, hardware test or original-binary comparison was performed; every independent build flag remains null.
- https://github.com/stewbc/mminer-apple2/blob/eabbc96695afda8ce8806e7da64a0c6624f50252/README.md

### Runtime profiles (reviewed)

Native HGR game with keyboard-toggle controls, optional scrolling modes, speaker audio and ProDOS exit/loader. README states the game uses $0800–$BE4B, but that range is not converted into a fabricated whole-system RAM minimum. No exact II/II+/IIe/IIc minimum or independent hardware result is asserted.

### AI (no-evidence-found)

No explicit development-AI disclosure was found in the inspected README, implementation, build and license sample or the available first-page commit messages (up to 20). Gameplay opponent AI, tool filenames and the age of the project are not development-AI evidence; usage stays unknown.

### Relationships (reviewed)

One native rewrite lineage covers scrolling and non-scrolling variants. Existing Spectrum and BBC Manic Miner projects are distinct implementations, not mirrors of this rewrite. LICENSE dedicates this code to the public domain; that does not independently clear Matthew Smith/Bug-Byte game art and other original-derived material. Normalized root, title/alias and lineage were screened against the 1,798-project f003aa64 catalogue, source/decision files and expanded 2,198-root prior union; no same-code catalogue entry was found.

- https://github.com/blondie7575/gscats/blob/5c9e8b47db5feaca545fc58662137fafe96a27d4/gamemanager.s

### Target CPU (reviewed)

The Makefile explicitly passes --cpu 65816 for the game, loader and fonts despite using the apple2enh platform setting. Banked native code and register-width macros support IIgs-native execution; it must not be mislabeled 65C02 because of that platform argument.
- https://github.com/blondie7575/gscats/blob/5c9e8b47db5feaca545fc58662137fafe96a27d4/catfight.s
- https://github.com/blondie7575/gscats/blob/5c9e8b47db5feaca545fc58662137fafe96a27d4/gamemanager.s
- https://github.com/blondie7575/gscats/blob/5c9e8b47db5feaca545fc58662137fafe96a27d4/Makefile

### Build (reviewed)

cl65/ca65, Cadius and Python generate code/fonts/title and an 800 KB image. Sprite and sound banks are committed; rebuilding art adds Mr. Sprite and the external sound pipeline. The default all target also launches an author-specific GSPlus path, so build and launch are not separable by blindly running make. Limitations: No compilation, launch, gameplay, hardware test or original-binary comparison was performed; every independent build flag remains null.
- https://github.com/blondie7575/gscats/blob/5c9e8b47db5feaca545fc58662137fafe96a27d4/ReadMe.md
- https://github.com/blondie7575/gscats/blob/5c9e8b47db5feaca545fc58662137fafe96a27d4/catfight.s
- https://github.com/blondie7575/gscats/blob/5c9e8b47db5feaca545fc58662137fafe96a27d4/gamemanager.s
- https://github.com/blondie7575/gscats/blob/5c9e8b47db5feaca545fc58662137fafe96a27d4/Makefile

### Runtime profiles (reviewed)

Author explicitly requires at least 512 KB RAM, stock unaccelerated IIgs support and ProDOS 8; the supplied image is 800 KB. The volume name is hardcoded in the loader, limiting casual relocation to hard disk. Reported ROM01 tests are upstream claims only.
- https://github.com/blondie7575/gscats/blob/5c9e8b47db5feaca545fc58662137fafe96a27d4/ReadMe.md
- https://github.com/blondie7575/gscats/blob/5c9e8b47db5feaca545fc58662137fafe96a27d4/catfight.s

- https://github.com/blondie7575/gscats/blob/5c9e8b47db5feaca545fc58662137fafe96a27d4/Makefile

No explicit development-AI disclosure was found in the inspected README, implementation, build and license sample or the available first-page commit messages (up to 20). Gameplay opponent AI, tool filenames and the age of the project are not development-AI evidence; usage stays unknown.

### Relationships (reviewed)

Canonical blondie7575 repository is one lineage; other GSCats forks are not added. No repository-wide LICENSE file exists in the complete tree. README separately attributes two sound samples under CC licenses; those notices do not establish a code license. Normalized root, title/alias and lineage were screened against the 1,798-project f003aa64 catalogue, source/decision files and expanded 2,198-root prior union; no same-code catalogue entry was found.

### Identity (reviewed)

Peter Hirschberg’s native IIgs recreation of the classic Adventure console game includes object/player state, wall/object collision, bat/dragon updates and selectable game modes in the game tick.
- https://github.com/peterhirschberg/gs.adventure/blob/0ce4f2c2b11e9393926676f7f6dd446b1bd5f648/README.md
- https://github.com/peterhirschberg/gs.adventure/blob/0ce4f2c2b11e9393926676f7f6dd446b1bd5f648/GS.Adventure/game.s
- https://github.com/peterhirschberg/gs.adventure/blob/0ce4f2c2b11e9393926676f7f6dd446b1bd5f648/GS.Adventure/main.c

### Classification (reviewed)

Native reverse-engineering-derived Adventure port. SPECIAL_THANKS.md explicitly says the author relied heavily on Joel D. Park and others’ annotated original Adventure assembly. The C/65816 implementation runs game state directly, rather than emulating an Atari CPU.
- https://github.com/peterhirschberg/gs.adventure/blob/0ce4f2c2b11e9393926676f7f6dd446b1bd5f648/README.md
- https://github.com/peterhirschberg/gs.adventure/blob/0ce4f2c2b11e9393926676f7f6dd446b1bd5f648/SPECIAL_THANKS.md

### Source CPU (no-evidence-found)

The README identifies the classic console Adventure and SPECIAL_THANKS.md identifies Atari’s 1980 Warren Robinett game and the annotated assembly predecessor. This establishes the historical Atari 2600 game lineage, but that external listing itself was not inspected, so no source ISA or original-source language is assigned as a verified input fact.
- https://github.com/peterhirschberg/gs.adventure/blob/0ce4f2c2b11e9393926676f7f6dd446b1bd5f648/README.md
- https://github.com/peterhirschberg/gs.adventure/blob/0ce4f2c2b11e9393926676f7f6dd446b1bd5f648/SPECIAL_THANKS.md

### Target CPU (reviewed)

game.s explicitly switches CPU mode with XCE and REP #$30 and uses native IIgs routines. This proves a 65816 output independently of the machine label.
- https://github.com/peterhirschberg/gs.adventure/blob/0ce4f2c2b11e9393926676f7f6dd446b1bd5f648/GS.Adventure/game.s

### Build (reviewed)

ORCA C/assembly/resource build pipeline and Xcode wrapper are present, targeting a desktop application with 320-pixel horizontal mode. External ORCA tools and local environment paths are required; no rebuilt application or resource package was verified. Limitations: No compilation, launch, gameplay, hardware test or original-binary comparison was performed; every independent build flag remains null.

### Identity (reviewed)

The original creators revived their unfinished 1991–1994 IIgs platform game, stitching historical levels into a not-quite-finished playable form and adding modern level tools. MAIN.S and PLAYER.S implement actual movement, world loading and scripted player behavior.
- https://github.com/gbirkel/merryo_trolls/blob/0799f2f56533ba844c39b45bd01bd053536a5902/README.md

- https://github.com/gbirkel/merryo_trolls/blob/0799f2f56533ba844c39b45bd01bd053536a5902/src/LIB/PLAYER.S

### Classification (reviewed)

Original-author historical homebrew restoration with modern tooling, rather than disassembly or a web remake. Browser editors manipulate the native game’s data but are not the game runtime.
- https://github.com/gbirkel/merryo_trolls/blob/0799f2f56533ba844c39b45bd01bd053536a5902/README.md
- https://github.com/gbirkel/merryo_trolls/blob/0799f2f56533ba844c39b45bd01bd053536a5902/src/MAIN.S
- https://github.com/gbirkel/merryo_trolls/blob/0799f2f56533ba844c39b45bd01bd053536a5902/src/LIB/PLAYER.S

### Source CPU (reviewed)

The author identifies these as the recovered native 1991–1994 game sources. Their XCE, REP/SEP and banked instructions substantiate a 65816 historical source, separately from the modern Python/browser tooling.
- https://github.com/gbirkel/merryo_trolls/blob/0799f2f56533ba844c39b45bd01bd053536a5902/README.md
- https://github.com/gbirkel/merryo_trolls/blob/0799f2f56533ba844c39b45bd01bd053536a5902/src/MAIN.S
- https://github.com/gbirkel/merryo_trolls/blob/0799f2f56533ba844c39b45bd01bd053536a5902/src/LIB/PLAYER.S

### Target CPU (reviewed)

MAIN.S and PLAYER.S use XCE, REP/SEP register-width changes, long banked addresses and IIgs SHR registers, directly establishing native 65816 output.
- https://github.com/gbirkel/merryo_trolls/blob/0799f2f56533ba844c39b45bd01bd053536a5902/src/MAIN.S
- https://github.com/gbirkel/merryo_trolls/blob/0799f2f56533ba844c39b45bd01bd053536a5902/src/LIB/PLAYER.S
- https://github.com/gbirkel/merryo_trolls/blob/0799f2f56533ba844c39b45bd01bd053536a5902/build.sh

### Build (reviewed)

build.sh is Python despite its suffix: it invokes Merlin32 and CiderPress II and assembles boot/world sources before packaging an 800 KB disk. Tool/library paths are placeholders requiring configuration; the script also imports gpxpy. No assembly or image creation was performed. Limitations: No compilation, launch, gameplay, hardware test or original-binary comparison was performed; every independent build flag remains null.
- https://github.com/gbirkel/merryo_trolls/blob/0799f2f56533ba844c39b45bd01bd053536a5902/README.md
- https://github.com/gbirkel/merryo_trolls/blob/0799f2f56533ba844c39b45bd01bd053536a5902/src/MAIN.S

- https://github.com/gbirkel/merryo_trolls/blob/0799f2f56533ba844c39b45bd01bd053536a5902/build.sh

### Runtime profiles (reviewed)

Upstream says the result can be written to an 800 KB 3.5-inch disk and played on stock IIgs with a joystick. It recommends cycle-accurate Crossrunner for the original timing and warns of prior emulator failures/speed problems. No numeric RAM or GS/OS minimum is supplied; the native source enables SHR.

### Identity (reviewed)

A native IIgs port of XSokoban using Lucas Scharenbroich’s Tool160 game engine. play.c implements legal push/move checks, move history/undo and level completion; main.c drives levels and score handling.
- https://github.com/vavruska/sokoban/blob/44b920db6884d74d5acbe8bb173b311ed44a2884/README.md
- https://github.com/vavruska/sokoban/blob/44b920db6884d74d5acbe8bb173b311ed44a2884/play.c
- https://github.com/vavruska/sokoban/blob/44b920db6884d74d5acbe8bb173b311ed44a2884/main.c
- https://github.com/vavruska/sokoban/blob/44b920db6884d74d5acbe8bb173b311ed44a2884/gte.h

### Classification (reviewed)

Source-led native C port of XSokoban. Tool160 supplies display/tool services while the repository contains the puzzle logic; it is not merely a collection of levels or a general emulator.
- https://github.com/vavruska/sokoban/blob/44b920db6884d74d5acbe8bb173b311ed44a2884/README.md
- https://github.com/vavruska/sokoban/blob/44b920db6884d74d5acbe8bb173b311ed44a2884/play.c
- https://github.com/vavruska/sokoban/blob/44b920db6884d74d5acbe8bb173b311ed44a2884/main.c
- https://github.com/vavruska/sokoban/blob/44b920db6884d74d5acbe8bb173b311ed44a2884/gte.h

### Source CPU (reviewed)

README identifies XSokoban as the source predecessor. The port preserves C rule logic, but no historical machine-code input or single XSokoban CPU architecture is evidenced; source_cpu is intentionally empty.
- https://github.com/vavruska/sokoban/blob/44b920db6884d74d5acbe8bb173b311ed44a2884/README.md
- https://github.com/vavruska/sokoban/blob/44b920db6884d74d5acbe8bb173b311ed44a2884/play.c

### Target CPU (no-evidence-found)

The reviewed makefile invokes occ and sets S16 metadata, while main.c calls IIgs Toolbox and gte.h uses the banked tool dispatcher. No explicit assembler CPU directive or output ISA option was inspected, so target_cpu remains empty rather than deriving 65816 solely from IIgs/S16 labels.
- https://github.com/vavruska/sokoban/blob/44b920db6884d74d5acbe8bb173b311ed44a2884/play.c
- https://github.com/vavruska/sokoban/blob/44b920db6884d74d5acbe8bb173b311ed44a2884/main.c

- https://github.com/vavruska/sokoban/blob/44b920db6884d74d5acbe8bb173b311ed44a2884/makefile

### Build (reviewed)

Makefile compiles C with occ, compiles Rez resources and uses iix to set S16 file metadata. Tool160 is a dependency and is present as a binary; building all its internals was not part of this review. Limitations: No compilation, launch, gameplay, hardware test or original-binary comparison was performed; every independent build flag remains null.
- https://github.com/vavruska/sokoban/blob/44b920db6884d74d5acbe8bb173b311ed44a2884/README.md

### Runtime profiles (reviewed)

IIgs application using Tool160/GTE, tile graphics and keyboard commands; README documents arrow or H/J/K/L input, run and push modifiers. No numeric memory floor, screen-mode dimensions or required GS/OS version is established in the reviewed files.

### Relationships (reviewed)

The README explicitly credits XSokoban as predecessor; no matching XSokoban game lineage exists in the screened catalogue. Root LICENSE is the Unlicense, but gte.h separately states Apache-2.0-derived GTE material. The root dedication should not be generalized to third-party XSokoban/GTE assets without their original notices. Normalized root, title/alias and lineage were screened against the 1,798-project f003aa64 catalogue, source/decision files and expanded 2,198-root prior union; no same-code catalogue entry was found.

### Identity (reviewed)

Realm is the author’s 1980s Apple II adventure, first released in 2021 after recovery. The current source contains substantive combat, world/town/dungeon code, maps and a native map interpreter; later releases substantially rework its assembly.
- https://github.com/dfgordon/realm/blob/c77a4ee3945935176eb3cd4f8619df8c5a502b6f/README.md
- https://github.com/dfgordon/realm/blob/c77a4ee3945935176eb3cd4f8619df8c5a502b6f/basic-common/COMBAT.bas
- https://github.com/dfgordon/realm/blob/c77a4ee3945935176eb3cd4f8619df8c5a502b6f/assembly/MAP.INTRP.S

### Classification (reviewed)

Original-author source recovery/restoration and continued native development. Legacy material is preserved beside modernized Applesoft and newly written assembly, rather than a binary-derived decompilation.
- https://github.com/dfgordon/realm/blob/c77a4ee3945935176eb3cd4f8619df8c5a502b6f/README.md
- https://github.com/dfgordon/realm/blob/c77a4ee3945935176eb3cd4f8619df8c5a502b6f/basic-common/COMBAT.bas
- https://github.com/dfgordon/realm/blob/c77a4ee3945935176eb3cd4f8619df8c5a502b6f/assembly/MAP.INTRP.S

### Source CPU (reviewed)

The author identifies recovered 1980s Apple II game code, while the inspected map interpreter is native 6502 assembly with zero-page addressing. The record distinguishes recovered BASIC/legacy source from the later rewritten assembly; it does not claim unchanged binary identity.
- https://github.com/dfgordon/realm/blob/c77a4ee3945935176eb3cd4f8619df8c5a502b6f/README.md
- https://github.com/dfgordon/realm/blob/c77a4ee3945935176eb3cd4f8619df8c5a502b6f/assembly/MAP.INTRP.S

### Target CPU (reviewed)

MAP.INTRP.S contains 6502 accumulator/X/Y operations, zero-page pointers and Apple II memory-oriented routines. This supports native 6502 output; no IIgs 65816 mode or 65C02 requirement is established.
- https://github.com/dfgordon/realm/blob/c77a4ee3945935176eb3cd4f8619df8c5a502b6f/basic-common/COMBAT.bas
- https://github.com/dfgordon/realm/blob/c77a4ee3945935176eb3cd4f8619df8c5a502b6f/assembly/MAP.INTRP.S
- https://github.com/dfgordon/realm/blob/c77a4ee3945935176eb3cd4f8619df8c5a502b6f/scripts/README.md
- https://github.com/dfgordon/realm/blob/c77a4ee3945935176eb3cd4f8619df8c5a502b6f/scripts/deploy.py

### Build (reviewed)

Python deployment scripts tokenize/package DOS 3.3 and ProDOS variants through a2kit. Current deploy.py checks Python >=3.12,<4 and a2kit >=4,<5. The tree contains assembly sources and packaged code, but a full fresh assembly-to-disk rebuild was not demonstrated by this static inspection. Limitations: No compilation, launch, gameplay, hardware test or original-binary comparison was performed; every independent build flag remains null.
- https://github.com/dfgordon/realm/blob/c77a4ee3945935176eb3cd4f8619df8c5a502b6f/README.md
- https://github.com/dfgordon/realm/blob/c77a4ee3945935176eb3cd4f8619df8c5a502b6f/basic-common/COMBAT.bas

README explicitly retains support for the original 48 KB Apple II Plus with one or two 5.25-inch drives. DOS 3.3 and ProDOS packages coexist, so the 48 KB claim is not indiscriminately assigned to every ProDOS configuration. No hardware run was independently performed.

### Identity (reviewed)

Escape is Christophe Meneboeuf’s Apple II roguelike project. The inspected game loop loads levels, accepts movement, runs tile reactions, updates the view and opens a map; the tree also contains procedural level generation and actor definitions.
- https://github.com/pixinn/rogue-like/blob/261b7276b5839246001facf25e4f0323bd423d60/README.md

- https://github.com/pixinn/rogue-like/blob/261b7276b5839246001facf25e4f0323bd423d60/doc/Doc.md

### Classification (reviewed)

Native homebrew game work in progress with real level/player state, not merely the author’s tile-engine article. Combat completeness, endgame and a complete adventure have not been established; scope is an exploratory roguelike prototype.
- https://github.com/pixinn/rogue-like/blob/261b7276b5839246001facf25e4f0323bd423d60/README.md
- https://github.com/pixinn/rogue-like/blob/261b7276b5839246001facf25e4f0323bd423d60/src/game_loop.asm

### Source CPU (not-applicable)

This is newly authored native game code rather than a recovered predecessor executable. No original-binary CPU is assigned from its target machine or the game that inspired it; source_platforms, source_cpu and source_language remain empty.
- https://github.com/pixinn/rogue-like/blob/261b7276b5839246001facf25e4f0323bd423d60/README.md

### Target CPU (reviewed)

Makefile explicitly passes --cpu 6502 alongside -t apple2. player.asm and game_loop.asm contain executable 6502 movement, pointer and dispatch logic.

### Build (reviewed)

GNU Make invokes cc65/cl65 assembly with a custom linker config; AppleCommander/Java packages the executable and level files into a disk image. Upstream says cc65 2.19 was known to build it, but this was not reproduced. Limitations: No compilation, launch, gameplay, hardware test or original-binary comparison was performed; every independent build flag remains null.

### Identity (reviewed)

Jeremy Rand’s independent C implementation of 2048 has board sliding/merging, scoring and animated text-page updates. It is distinct from David Schmidt’s Atari-engine-derived AII2048 port.
- https://github.com/jeremysrand/apple2048/blob/1fdc2586afa6899b2817261681d4eb21e77f366e/README.md
- https://github.com/jeremysrand/apple2048/blob/1fdc2586afa6899b2817261681d4eb21e77f366e/apple2048/game.c
- https://github.com/jeremysrand/apple2048/blob/1fdc2586afa6899b2817261681d4eb21e77f366e/apple2048/textpage.s

### Classification (reviewed)

Native homebrew interpretation of 2048 rules. The C game.c implements its own board logic; optional IIgs assembly fixes a display-page shadowing issue without emulating a CPU.
- https://github.com/jeremysrand/apple2048/blob/1fdc2586afa6899b2817261681d4eb21e77f366e/README.md
- https://github.com/jeremysrand/apple2048/blob/1fdc2586afa6899b2817261681d4eb21e77f366e/apple2048/game.c
- https://github.com/jeremysrand/apple2048/blob/1fdc2586afa6899b2817261681d4eb21e77f366e/apple2048/textpage.s

### Source CPU (not-applicable)

This is newly authored native game code rather than a recovered predecessor executable. No original-binary CPU is assigned from its target machine or the game that inspired it; source_platforms, source_cpu and source_language remain empty.
- https://github.com/jeremysrand/apple2048/blob/1fdc2586afa6899b2817261681d4eb21e77f366e/README.md
- https://github.com/jeremysrand/apple2048/blob/1fdc2586afa6899b2817261681d4eb21e77f366e/apple2048/game.c
- https://github.com/jeremysrand/apple2048/blob/1fdc2586afa6899b2817261681d4eb21e77f366e/apple2048/textpage.s

### Target CPU (reviewed)

textpage.s selects .PC02 and uses STZ for the normal 65C02 path; its IIgs-only routine selects .P816, executes XCE/REP and writes to bank $E0. The Makefile default of CPU=65816 permits that mixed source, but does not make every machine require a native IIgs CPU.
- https://github.com/jeremysrand/apple2048/blob/1fdc2586afa6899b2817261681d4eb21e77f366e/apple2048/game.c
- https://github.com/jeremysrand/apple2048/blob/1fdc2586afa6899b2817261681d4eb21e77f366e/apple2048/textpage.s
- https://github.com/jeremysrand/apple2048/blob/1fdc2586afa6899b2817261681d4eb21e77f366e/apple2048/Makefile
- https://github.com/jeremysrand/apple2048/blob/1fdc2586afa6899b2817261681d4eb21e77f366e/apple2048/make/head.mk

### Build (reviewed)

cc65 build/image pipeline and AppleCommander jar are present. README explicitly says the code no longer builds with latest cc65 because the text-frame function was removed; a compatible older toolchain or replacement routine is needed. No failed or successful build was independently run. Limitations: No compilation, launch, gameplay, hardware test or original-binary comparison was performed; every independent build flag remains null.
- https://github.com/jeremysrand/apple2048/blob/1fdc2586afa6899b2817261681d4eb21e77f366e/README.md

### Runtime profiles (reviewed)

README requires enhanced Apple IIe, IIc or IIgs and a ProDOS system file. The IIgs path directly copies text page two into $E0, avoiding the Alternate Display Mode dependency. No numeric RAM floor is claimed.

Native cc65 C homebrew game. Its HGR implementation writes the Apple II framebuffer and soft switches, rather than emulating hardware. Alpha status is preserved; no completion/fidelity claim is inferred from the shipped disk.

### Identity (reviewed)

A turn-based Apple II strategy game with production chains, trade, diplomacy, research, ships and a Council of Nations victory/score flow. logic.c implements turn advancement and resource/economic rules; assembly supports native graphics.
- https://github.com/taciano-perez/iimperialism/blob/fd3585f97b32cfcb038b4c01d8b11fd1376b1276/README.md
- https://github.com/taciano-perez/iimperialism/blob/fd3585f97b32cfcb038b4c01d8b11fd1376b1276/src/logic.c
- https://github.com/taciano-perez/iimperialism/blob/fd3585f97b32cfcb038b4c01d8b11fd1376b1276/asm/text_hgr.s

### Classification (reviewed)

Original native homebrew inspired by Imperialism and Taipan!, with resident code and 2 KB overlays. It is not a binary reconstruction of either inspiration and does not use a host emulator as its game engine.
- https://github.com/taciano-perez/iimperialism/blob/fd3585f97b32cfcb038b4c01d8b11fd1376b1276/README.md
- https://github.com/taciano-perez/iimperialism/blob/fd3585f97b32cfcb038b4c01d8b11fd1376b1276/src/logic.c

### Source CPU (not-applicable)

This is newly authored native game code rather than a recovered predecessor executable. No original-binary CPU is assigned from its target machine or the game that inspired it; source_platforms, source_cpu and source_language remain empty.

- https://github.com/taciano-perez/iimperialism/blob/fd3585f97b32cfcb038b4c01d8b11fd1376b1276/src/logic.c
- https://github.com/taciano-perez/iimperialism/blob/fd3585f97b32cfcb038b4c01d8b11fd1376b1276/asm/text_hgr.s

### Target CPU (reviewed)

README explicitly describes Apple II-compatible 6502 software including the IIgs compatibility case. text_hgr.s contains direct 6502 rendering and cc65 stack/zero-page code; the IIgs is not a separate 65816 output.
- https://github.com/taciano-perez/iimperialism/blob/fd3585f97b32cfcb038b4c01d8b11fd1376b1276/src/logic.c
- https://github.com/taciano-perez/iimperialism/blob/fd3585f97b32cfcb038b4c01d8b11fd1376b1276/asm/text_hgr.s
- https://github.com/taciano-perez/iimperialism/blob/fd3585f97b32cfcb038b4c01d8b11fd1376b1276/Makefile

### Build (reviewed)

Makefile builds C, native assembly, overlays and disk bootstrap with cc65, ACME, qboot/ProRWTS and AppleCommander. README provides a Windows helper to restore third_party tools; that directory is absent from the inspected tree, so a fresh disk build has additional dependencies. No build script was executed. Limitations: No compilation, launch, gameplay, hardware test or original-binary comparison was performed; every independent build flag remains null.
- https://github.com/taciano-perez/iimperialism/blob/fd3585f97b32cfcb038b4c01d8b11fd1376b1276/README.md

- https://github.com/taciano-perez/iimperialism/blob/fd3585f97b32cfcb038b4c01d8b11fd1376b1276/Makefile

### Runtime profiles (reviewed)

Upstream explicitly requires 64 KB RAM, HGR and a compatible 5.25-inch/Disk II-style floppy path. It names IIe, IIc, IIgs in compatibility mode and expanded II/II+/Europlus; 48 KB configurations are explicitly unsupported. Custom qboot/ProRWTS handles boot, overlays and fixed 1024-byte saves; auxiliary-memory overlay caching is optional.

### AI (reviewed)

README explicitly says LLMs were used to develop the game and names OpenAI Codex and Claude Code, with model labels supplied by the author. This is direct project disclosure, not inference from configuration files or gameplay AI.

No matching lineage in the catalogue. MIT LICENSE covers this project’s code; README separately credits fonts derived from Taipan!, nation names from Imperialism, and external boot/tool dependencies. The game’s inspiration and borrowed assets do not imply source/binary identity. README says to use the provided disk or paste seed.bas into an emulator. There is no compiled build recipe or explicit license in the complete three-file tree. Applesoft BASIC text/menus using arrows or numeric choices; author advises pen and paper. The script calls Apple ROM routines and reads ROM locations, but exact ROM revision, RAM floor and all-model compatibility are not established.

### Identity (reviewed)

Stefan Wessels’ native Apple II Penetrator recreates the Spectrum side-scrolling shooter with player/enemy/projectile/world state and a built-in terrain editor. The root consolidates its earlier Commander X16, Apple II, Plus/4 and C64 editions.
- https://github.com/stewbc/penetrator/blob/dd6760bce30ccb1c8111250a7328d7df09a06a5c/README.md
- https://github.com/stewbc/penetrator/blob/dd6760bce30ccb1c8111250a7328d7df09a06a5c/README.apple2.md
- https://github.com/stewbc/penetrator/blob/dd6760bce30ccb1c8111250a7328d7df09a06a5c/src/apple2/penetrator.asm
- https://github.com/stewbc/penetrator/blob/dd6760bce30ccb1c8111250a7328d7df09a06a5c/src/apple2/game.inc

### Classification (reviewed)

Native game remake and source port. The Apple II edition was adapted from the author’s Commander X16 remake with rewritten drawing code; it is not a Spectrum CPU emulator or separate copy of the old penetrator-apple2 root.
- https://github.com/stewbc/penetrator/blob/dd6760bce30ccb1c8111250a7328d7df09a06a5c/README.md
- https://github.com/stewbc/penetrator/blob/dd6760bce30ccb1c8111250a7328d7df09a06a5c/README.apple2.md
- https://github.com/stewbc/penetrator/blob/dd6760bce30ccb1c8111250a7328d7df09a06a5c/src/apple2/penetrator.asm
- https://github.com/stewbc/penetrator/blob/dd6760bce30ccb1c8111250a7328d7df09a06a5c/src/apple2/game.inc

### Source CPU (no-evidence-found)

The root and Apple II READMEs identify the Spectrum game and the author’s Commander X16 remake as predecessors. This pass inspected the Apple II adaptation, not original Spectrum machine code or the X16 implementation; source_cpu remains unknown rather than inferred from either platform.
- https://github.com/stewbc/penetrator/blob/dd6760bce30ccb1c8111250a7328d7df09a06a5c/README.md
- https://github.com/stewbc/penetrator/blob/dd6760bce30ccb1c8111250a7328d7df09a06a5c/README.apple2.md

### Target CPU (reviewed)

Apple II README explicitly identifies 6502 assembly and ca65. penetrator.asm/game.inc implement native 6502 game state with Apple II includes and direct rendering calls.

Makefile has actual cx16/apple2/plus4/c64 targets; Apple II instructions cover loader, ca65/cl65 and AppleCommander/Java disk packaging. No edition was built. Missing original attract mode and fireworks are explicitly documented. Limitations: No compilation, launch, gameplay, hardware test or original-binary comparison was performed; every independent build flag remains null.

### Identity (reviewed)

Sean Callahan’s Apple II Kaboom! ports algorithms from an included Atari 2600 ROM disassembly, with native buckets/bombs/bomber graphics, paddle input and one- or two-player scoring.
- https://github.com/seanahan5000/kaboom/blob/08924d59c58b4c2988f80912d90222b5c8c9b205/README.md
- https://github.com/seanahan5000/kaboom/blob/08924d59c58b4c2988f80912d90222b5c8c9b205/src/kaboom.s
- https://github.com/seanahan5000/kaboom/blob/08924d59c58b4c2988f80912d90222b5c8c9b205/vcs/kaboom.a

### Classification (reviewed)

Reverse-engineering-derived native game port. The included vcs/kaboom.a is the 6502 reference disassembly; src/kaboom.s replaces console hardware paths with Apple II-native timing/input/rendering, rather than running the ROM in a hardware emulator.
- https://github.com/seanahan5000/kaboom/blob/08924d59c58b4c2988f80912d90222b5c8c9b205/README.md
- https://github.com/seanahan5000/kaboom/blob/08924d59c58b4c2988f80912d90222b5c8c9b205/src/kaboom.s
- https://github.com/seanahan5000/kaboom/blob/08924d59c58b4c2988f80912d90222b5c8c9b205/vcs/kaboom.a

### Source CPU (reviewed)

README identifies the included vcs/kaboom.a as disassembled Atari 2600 ROM code used directly for algorithms. The listing explicitly says processor 6502 and preserves original address/byte comments, establishing 6502 input separately from Apple II output. Here 6502 is the instruction-set family: the Atari 2600’s 6507 hardware variant is not distinguished by seeing these opcodes, and the record makes no full-package 6502 hardware claim.
- https://github.com/seanahan5000/kaboom/blob/08924d59c58b4c2988f80912d90222b5c8c9b205/README.md
- https://github.com/seanahan5000/kaboom/blob/08924d59c58b4c2988f80912d90222b5c8c9b205/vcs/kaboom.a

### Target CPU (reviewed)

The reference disassembly explicitly declares processor 6502. Native Apple II assembly uses the same instruction family plus Apple II screen/keyboard/paddle memory locations; no 65C02/65816-only requirement is established.
- https://github.com/seanahan5000/kaboom/blob/08924d59c58b4c2988f80912d90222b5c8c9b205/src/kaboom.s
- https://github.com/seanahan5000/kaboom/blob/08924d59c58b4c2988f80912d90222b5c8c9b205/vcs/kaboom.a
- https://github.com/seanahan5000/kaboom/blob/08924d59c58b4c2988f80912d90222b5c8c9b205/build.sh

### Build (reviewed)

build.sh invokes ca65 and ld65 with an Apple II linker config; assembly source, listing and disk image are present. The trailing RPW-debugger copy step assumes an external local project layout. No assembly, disk boot or binary match was performed. Limitations: No compilation, launch, gameplay, hardware test or original-binary comparison was performed; every independent build flag remains null.
- https://github.com/seanahan5000/kaboom/blob/08924d59c58b4c2988f80912d90222b5c8c9b205/README.md

- https://github.com/seanahan5000/kaboom/blob/08924d59c58b4c2988f80912d90222b5c8c9b205/build.sh

### Runtime profiles (reviewed)

Paddles are the gameplay controls; Mockingboard is required only for sound. Accurate floating-bus/vapor-lock emulation is required or the game can hang. Upstream specifically says MicroM8 needs Cycle Accurate Render enabled. No numeric RAM or exact Apple II model floor is stated.

No explicit development-AI disclosure was found in the inspected README, implementation, build and license sample or the available first-page commit messages (up to 20). Gameplay opponent AI, tool filenames and the age of the project are not development-AI evidence; usage stays unknown.

One native Apple II port plus its Atari reference disassembly. The README describes direct algorithm/bitmap derivation from the Atari ROM; no LICENSE file exists and the original game rights are not cleared by source publication. Normalized root, title/alias and lineage were screened against the 1,798-project f003aa64 catalogue, source/decision files and expanded 2,198-root prior union; no same-code catalogue entry was found. The catalogue’s Dennis Debro Atari disassembly collection also covers Kaboom; that is reference-game overlap, not this separate Apple II hardware adaptation, and no duplicate original-disassembly entry is added.

### Identity (reviewed)

Stephan Gay preserves and reconstructs Dale Johnson’s 1983 Phoenix Software Apple II adventure. The reviewed second attempt implements entity state, commands, inventory, movement, timed turns and game-end handling.
- https://github.com/zpqrtbnk/masquerade/blob/ab86a44968ba09034a75384da27be9cdb41831d4/README.md

### Classification (reviewed)

Binary-derived analysis and direct JavaScript implementation. Game handlers are mapped to original routine addresses; retained Bobbin/Ghidra tools serve analysis and are not the browser game’s execution engine. The first attempted port is expressly described as failed; this record evaluates attempt.2.
- https://github.com/zpqrtbnk/masquerade/blob/ab86a44968ba09034a75384da27be9cdb41831d4/README.md

The source analysis identifies 6502 instructions, zero-page state, JMP/JSR routine tables and Apple II memory addresses. This establishes 6502 input independently of the modern JavaScript output.

The selected output is an ECMAScript-module browser game. No fixed host instruction-set requirement is declared; target_cpu stays empty, rather than inheriting the original 6502.

### Identity (reviewed)

Open Sosaria targets Richard Garriott’s original 1981 Apple II Ultima, rather than the later rewritten Ultima I release. Source implements dungeon movement, monsters, the Mondain encounter, disk decoding and other game scenes.
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/README.md
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/src/entities/playerDungeon.c
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/src/entities/mondain.c
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/src/scenes/sceneDiskLoader.c

### Classification (reviewed)

Native C game-engine recreation informed by the original disks and disassembly. vmExecuter.c is a wait/scene/message command queue, not a 6502 CPU interpreter. Original graphics and BASIC data are decoded, while gameplay is implemented directly.
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/src/entities/vmExecuter.c
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/src/entities/playerDungeon.c
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/src/scenes/sceneDiskLoader.c

### Source CPU (reviewed)

The README explicitly identifies disassembled 6502 delay loops as the source for audio behavior, and the loader parses original Applesoft files and Apple II disk structures. 6502 belongs to the input implementation, not the modern host.
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/README.md
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/src/scenes/sceneDiskLoader.c
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/src/engine/audio.c

### Target CPU (reviewed)

BUILDING.md explicitly prescribes the MSYS2 ucrt-x86_64 toolchain for Windows, supporting x86-64 as one declared target. Linux/macOS Makefile routes do not specify their output ISA, so ARM64 or additional architectures are not inferred.
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/BUILDING.md
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/Makefile

### Build (reviewed)

GNU Make builds C99 with GLFW3, OpenGL, glad and miniaudio. Original game disk images must be supplied. The README’s planned bin/disk1.dsk and bin/disk2.dsk convention conflicts with the actual loader, which opens program.dsk and player.dsk relative to the working directory; code governs this finding. Limitations: No candidate was compiled, executed, played or byte-compared. Independent compilation, runnability, playability and byte-exactness remain null.
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/Makefile
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/BUILDING.md
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/README.md
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/src/scenes/sceneDiskLoader.c

### Runtime profiles (reviewed)

Upstream claims v0.6.0 is beatable, but notes deliberate timing reinterpretation and a ladder-down soft-lock at dungeon level 10. No independent fidelity check, precise OS version or RAM minimum is established. The two legal game disks and an OpenGL-capable host remain dependencies.
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/README.md
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/docs/engine-reinterpretation-notes.md
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/src/scenes/sceneDiskLoader.c

No explicit development-AI attribution was found in the inspected README, build and implementation files or the 100 newest commit messages. That bounded negative search does not establish non-use.
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/README.md
- https://github.com/delcodigo/open-sosaria/blob/578828dc506dc9d3dd6931783ab66e1849642bad/src/entities/playerDungeon.c

### Relationships (reviewed)

Independent C engine, distinct from the indexed XekriRedmane Apple II disassembly. MIT applies to this project’s code; original Ultima disk images must be legally supplied and are not granted by that license. No matching Open Sosaria root was present in the current/prior union.

### Identity (reviewed)

Viacheslav Chimishuk’s loderunner-ng implements the classic Apple II Lode Runner game with 150 selectable levels. Inspected C files include digging, movement, guard interaction, game state, scoring and lives.

### Classification (reviewed)

A mostly-from-scratch C implementation using Total Recall as a behavioral reference, as explicitly stated upstream. The reviewed runner/game implementation directly updates game state through SDL2; no 6502 execution core is involved.

Separate emulator and legacy routes exist in the same repository; those are audit context and are not additional game projects.

### Source CPU (reviewed)

Recovered BASIC uses Apple II PEEK/POKE, PDL and CALL-based graphics, with accompanying 6502 opcode listings. The inspected SPACE SIMULATOR listing starts with retained DOS header bytes and is not accepted as byte-exact assembly source; later routines still establish 6502 input.
- https://github.com/plicerin/space-vikings-resurrected/blob/c4d9861515bbb97b9dac9031411a75fd6edc8163/detokenized/STARSHIP%20SIMULATOR.bas
- https://github.com/plicerin/space-vikings-resurrected/blob/c4d9861515bbb97b9dac9031411a75fd6edc8163/disassembly/SPACE%20SIMULATOR%20ASSEMBLY.asm

### Target CPU (reviewed)

modern/web builds TypeScript/JavaScript for a browser. No fixed host ISA is declared; the old 6502 is an analysis input rather than the selected output CPU.
- https://github.com/plicerin/space-vikings-resurrected/blob/c4d9861515bbb97b9dac9031411a75fd6edc8163/modern/web/package.json
- https://github.com/plicerin/space-vikings-resurrected/blob/c4d9861515bbb97b9dac9031411a75fd6edc8163/modern/web/src/main.ts

### Build (reviewed)

The selected web package defines tsc followed by Vite build, using TypeScript ^5.6.3 and Vite ^5.4.10. Root handover prose predates the modern implementation and is not used to deny the code now present. Source tools and tests were inspected, never run. Limitations: No candidate was compiled, executed, played or byte-compared. Independent compilation, runnability, playability and byte-exactness remain null.
- https://github.com/plicerin/space-vikings-resurrected/blob/c4d9861515bbb97b9dac9031411a75fd6edc8163/modern/web/package.json
- https://github.com/plicerin/space-vikings-resurrected/blob/c4d9861515bbb97b9dac9031411a75fd6edc8163/modern/web/README.md
- https://github.com/plicerin/space-vikings-resurrected/blob/c4d9861515bbb97b9dac9031411a75fd6edc8163/README.md

### Runtime profiles (reviewed)

The author’s checklist still leaves the headed end-to-end scene-flow check unfinished; gameState retains unresolved address labels, and cockpit has fallback artwork when bytecode assets are absent. Modern browser gameplay and fidelity remain unverified. No historical RAM minimum or precise modern browser version is inferred.
- https://github.com/plicerin/space-vikings-resurrected/blob/c4d9861515bbb97b9dac9031411a75fd6edc8163/modern/web/TODO-modern-remake.md
- https://github.com/plicerin/space-vikings-resurrected/blob/c4d9861515bbb97b9dac9031411a75fd6edc8163/modern/web/src/engine/gameState.ts
- https://github.com/plicerin/space-vikings-resurrected/blob/c4d9861515bbb97b9dac9031411a75fd6edc8163/modern/web/src/scenes/cockpit.ts

### AI (reviewed)

Actual implementation and recovery commits carry explicit Copilot coauthor trailers. This supports project-level AI use for recovery/tool implementation, without claiming Copilot authored every modern TypeScript line. The seven available commit messages were inspected; image filenames alone are not used as proof.
- https://github.com/plicerin/space-vikings-resurrected/blob/c4d9861515bbb97b9dac9031411a75fd6edc8163/README.md
- https://github.com/Plicerin/Space-Vikings-Resurrected/commit/a12f5fdb0354477ef565688bdfeea47a5056d908
- https://github.com/Plicerin/Space-Vikings-Resurrected/commit/9aadb8633bd45d74561191ef10947eb883010b81

### Relationships (reviewed)

One project covers extraction, partial disassembly, legacy emulator experiments and the selected modern remake. No matching root/lineage was indexed. No license grant was found in the inspected root and modern subtree; original disk assets remain rights-restricted. The repository-wide recursive tree request failed, so license absence is bounded to the inspected subtrees.

- https://github.com/plicerin/space-vikings-resurrected/blob/c4d9861515bbb97b9dac9031411a75fd6edc8163/modern/web/README.md

## Research event notes

User-approved Apple II and Apple IIgs game-source publication. Direct-main publication and live catalogue/feed verification authorized for these 35 consolidated repository-root projects. 35 proposed game projects across 35 canonical repositories: seven historical-source projects including one three-game author collection, twenty native games or restorations, and eight modern-host reconstructions/remakes. Baseline f003aa641b1120e74039742d26b483bb04199bf4 has 1,798 projects and 100 Git-blob-verified files. A union of 2,198 current catalogue/source/decision and prior-reviewed normalized GitHub roots was screened before primary work, with title/code-lineage checks and canonical fork/successor consolidation. Independent implementations can share historical titles. Apple II 6502/65C02 and Apple IIgs 65816 outputs remain distinct; CPU, RAM, display, disk and OS requirements are only assigned when supported. No game was compiled, run, played or byte-compared; all four independent verification flags stay null. Development-AI attribution requires explicit statements or implementation commits. Game-specific hardware-emulation companions are audit-only, while directly implemented game routes are evaluated separately. Original assets and source licensing limitations remain explicit. One Taipan README is CP1252 and uses a pinned URL plus separately hash-verified original bytes rather than an invalid UTF-8 cache proof. Coverage is bounded, search-only leads are not additions, and holds are preserved. All pre-existing records, audit objects, activity and state remain untouched.
